# Known Limitations & Open Decisions

This page consolidates known limitations and deferred design decisions surfaced
by the ongoing code review (see [review-log.md](./review-log.md) for the raw
findings and rotation). Items here are **intentionally open** — they need an
owner decision or are accepted trade-offs — rather than undocumented bugs. When
one is resolved, move it to the relevant module doc / CHANGELOG and remove it
here.

> Severity reflects review classification, not an SLA. "Open decision" means a
> reasonable person could choose either way; "Accepted" means we've decided to
> live with it for now.

> **Drift check, 2026-08-07.** A sweep of the MED-severity rows found several
> describing code that had since been fixed — the CSRF no-cookie branch, the
> role grant ceiling and last-admin guard (ORU-7), the skills-test
> self-certification half of CS-8, the shared-device PII purge (FE-6/FE-7), and
> the black pin drift. The fixes landed; the rows did not get updated. That
> direction of staleness is the dangerous one: this page is what a compliance
> reviewer reads and what the next audit uses to aim, so overstating open risk
> misdirects effort and understates the product.
>
> Staleness cuts both ways, though, and the sweep itself proved it. FIN-4 was
> initially marked resolved here on the strength of `assert_different_person`
> appearing in `finance_service` — but that guard sits on the **approval** step,
> not on disbursement, so `mark_pr_paid` / `issue_check` / `waive_dues` remain
> gated by `finance.manage` alone. The row is back to ⚠️ Narrowed. Read the
> call site, not the import: "the guard exists in this file" is not the same
> claim as "this path is guarded."
>
> Rows are annotated with the date they were last _verified against the code_,
> not just the date they were written. When you fix something listed here,
> update its row in the same change.

| **CSRF "no csrf cookie → allow" branch**                                                                                         | ✅ Resolved (verified 2026-08-07)                                                | The branch was tightened and this row had not been updated. `verify_csrf_token` now splits the no-cookie case: a request carrying an `access_token` cookie but no `csrf_token` is **rejected** (403 "Missing CSRF token") as anomalous, and only a request with no session cookie at all — unauthenticated, or a Bearer client whose header browsers never auto-send and which is therefore not CSRF-exploitable — is allowed through. `SameSite=Strict` remains the primary defense. (`security_middleware.py`.) | **`REGISTRATION_REQUIRES_APPROVAL` has no reader anywhere — every self-registered account is immediately active**                | 🚩 Open (HIGH, 2026-09-07, security review CI3-33)                               | `settings.REGISTRATION_REQUIRES_APPROVAL` (`config.py:300`, default `True`) is never read by any code path — confirmed by an exhaustive grep of `app/` for the name. There is also no admin-approval mechanism to read it into: `UserStatus` (`models/user.py`) has no pending/unapproved value, and `AuthService.register_user()` (`auth_service.py:446`) unconditionally creates the row with `status=UserStatus.ACTIVE`, after which `POST /auth/register` (`auth.py:559`) immediately issues access/refresh tokens and logs the caller in. The docstring's claim — "new accounts require admin approval if REGISTRATION_REQUIRES_APPROVAL is true" — describes a control that does not exist in code. Gated behind `REGISTRATION_ENABLED` (also default `False`), so this is latent unless an operator turns on self-registration — but that is exactly the deployment shape the approval flag exists to protect, and it silently does nothing. `docs/app-review/auth-session.md`'s prior note ("honored server-side but no admin queue in the UI") was itself wrong and has been corrected. Needs a product decision (a pending-approval `UserStatus`/flag plus an admin approval queue, or explicitly retiring the setting) — not a drive-by fix. See `docs/security-review/CI3-33-core-infra.md` (CI3-33-3). | **Several `config.py` settings have no reader anywhere in the backend**                                                          | 🚩 Open (LOW, 2026-09-07, security review CI3-33)                                | Beyond the two rows above, an exhaustive per-field reference sweep of `app/core/config.py` found more declared-and-documented settings with zero readers outside their own declaration: `RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` (actual limits are hardcoded per-scope in `security_middleware.py`'s `rate_limit_*()` helpers and the separate `RATE_LIMIT_DEFAULT` string, which _is_ read), `MAX_FILE_SIZE` and `STORAGE_TYPE` (`documents_service.py`/`documents.py` hardcode their own `UPLOAD_DIR` and size handling independently of these), and `DB_POOL_MIN` (only `DB_POOL_MAX` reaches `create_async_engine`). None of these gate a security control the way `REGISTRATION_REQUIRES_APPROVAL` does — they are tuning knobs an operator would set expecting an effect and silently get none. `LDAP_*` is a separate, already-documented case (CLAUDE.md: "exists in config but gates nothing — LDAP is not implemented"). See `docs/security-review/CI3-33-core-infra.md` (CI3-33-4). | **`RateLimiter` tracked per-key state across three independent dicts — resolved by a structural refactor to one per-key record** | ✅ Resolved (2026-09-07, security review CI3-33, PR #2370 round 3)               | `self.requests`, `self.lockouts`, and `self._key_windows` (`security_middleware.py`) were three separately-capped, separately-evicted structures meant to describe the same key's state, and seven successive Codex review rounds across two PRs (#2368, then #2370) each found a new way they — or a saturation-accuracy mechanism bolted onto the outside of that shape — drifted or misbehaved: CI3-33-1/2 (forced eviction silently lifting an active lockout / undercounting a key's own history), CI3-33-1a (window metadata not restored alongside history), CI3-33-1b/1c (`self.lockouts` uncapped, then its by-expiry eviction itself releasing an unrelated key's active lockout), CI3-33-1d/1e (`self._key_windows` uncapped independent of the other two; a saturated-table violator's only remaining protection being the much shorter request-history window rather than its lockout duration), CI3-33-2a/2b (a new saturation-reject signal was a single global scalar instead of being scoped per rate-limit scope — a cross-scope DoS — and a strict `>` eviction-gate comparison let a stale, already-expired lockout count trigger an unnecessary saturation rejection), and CI3-33-2c/2d (the fix for the previous round forced a full three-dict sweep on every request once the lockout table merely reached capacity — a CPU-amplification DoS — and, narrowed once, still let a single already-rejected key's own retries repeatedly re-trigger the capacity scan). Round seven (CI3-33-2d) was the immediately preceding fix's own commit found to have a narrower version of the exact gap it had just closed — the trigger this row previously named for stopping incremental patches and doing the refactor instead. **Resolved:** `backend/app/core/security_middleware.py`'s `RateLimiter` now stores one record per key — a small `_KeyState` dataclass (`request_times`, `window_seconds`, `lockout_until`) in a single `dict[str, _KeyState]` — so a key's request history, window, and lockout status can no longer exist in one structure but not another, closing the CI3-33-1-through-1e defect shape by construction. The separate capacity-accuracy problem (CI3-33-2b/2c/2d) needed a second idea beyond the merge: a cached `_active_lockout_count`, incremented on insertion and corrected exactly by the periodic sweep plus a narrow, throttled `_refresh_active_lockout_count` (at most once per second, far shorter than the 60-second general sweep) — bounding the cost of an accurate saturation answer to a fixed per-second rate regardless of whether the repeated asks come from one key retrying or many different keys arriving together, closing CI3-33-2b, 2c, and 2d together. `self._saturation_reject_until` (CI3-33-2a's per-scope dict) is deliberately not folded into `_KeyState` — it is keyed by a fixed, finite set of scope literals, a fundamentally different key space from the attacker-influenceable per-client keys the other three structures used. All 33 existing `TestRateLimiter` tests were read as a behavior spec and rewritten against the new shape, passing in full on the first run against the new class; 2 new tests (`TestRateLimiter` now 35 at that point) close CI3-33-2d directly and, via a seeded property-style fuzz test hammering the limiter across scopes/saturation/retries, verify the class-level invariants (internal state stays within documented bounds, `is_rate_limited`'s return value never disagrees with the state it leaves behind) rather than one more single-scenario regression test. **Round 8 (2026-09-07, PR #2370 round 4, CI3-33-2e/2f):** Codex review of the merged refactor found `_saturation_reject_until` itself had no size cap or eviction — safe only because every _current_ call site passes a literal scope, not because the interface enforced it (Pitfall #9). Fixed with a `_MAX_SATURATION_SCOPES` cap and eviction folded into the existing `_sweep` pass (expired entries first, then soonest-to-expire); 2 more tests (`TestRateLimiter` now 37). Two other round-8 threads (a zero-`lockout_seconds` retry variant of CI3-33-2d, a stale-capacity variant of CI3-33-2b) were verified moot against the refactor and needed no change. **Round 8 continued (PR #2370 round 5, CI3-33-2g/2h):** the same Codex pass found the `_MAX_KEYS` enforcement itself compared the _combined_ locked-out + unlocked key count against the flat cap — and the default config sets `_MAX_KEYS == _MAX_LOCKOUTS`, so a fully saturated table left zero room for even one unlocked key, forcing a real `O(N log N)` sweep+sort on every single retry from one attacker (CI3-33-2g) and, worse, letting an attacker alternating between two keys evict each other's one-entry history before either could trip its own lockout — a functional rate-limit **bypass** (CI3-33-2h). Fixed by budgeting `_MAX_KEYS` against _unlocked_ keys specifically (threshold `_MAX_KEYS + self._active_lockout_count`, not a flat `_MAX_KEYS`) — a genuinely active lockout was never evictable anyway, so excluding it from the budget costs nothing, while an unlocked key only faces eviction pressure once unlocked keys are actually crowding the table. **Round 9:** a third session collision on this exact finding (same pattern as CI3-33-2d) — a concurrent session independently found and fixed CI3-33-2g/2h too, sizing the eviction off an exact evictable-set scan rather than the cached `_active_lockout_count` approximation; verified equally correct and marginally more robust (immune to a stale count under-sizing the removal), so that version landed instead. Unlike the CI3-33-2d collision, both sessions' test additions merged with no conflict this time, so all 4 new tests are in the final suite (`TestRateLimiter` now 41, was 37). **Round 10 (CI3-33-2i):** a `lockout_seconds=0` insertion (the default for several public, unauthenticated endpoints) unconditionally incremented the _shared, cross-scope_ `_active_lockout_count`, even though a zero-duration lockout is never genuinely active — letting a public-endpoint flood of phantom entries push a completely unrelated scope's real violator (e.g. `login`) into the saturation fallback instead of its own per-key lockout. Fixed by excluding `lockout_seconds <= 0` insertions from the counter and the capacity check entirely. **Round 11:** a fourth session collision on this same finding — a concurrent session's smaller, one-line-gate fix (`if lockout_seconds > 0: self._active_lockout_count += 1`, leaving the capacity-checked branch structure untouched) landed instead of this session's own branch restructuring, verified equivalent; this round's test additions conflicted outright (same location in the file), so the other session's replaced this session's own (`TestRateLimiter` still 42, a swap not an addition). **Round 12 (CI3-33-2j) — the most severe finding in this class:** extending `self._saturation_reject_until[scope]` from a count merely _observed_ at capacity, without confirming it was accurate as of that call, let a sub-second stale-read race (the real table emptied moments before the check) commit a full `lockout_seconds` (often 30-minute), scope-wide rejection that never self-corrected — every fresh, no-history client in the scope stayed blocked for the full duration even once a later call proved real capacity had been available the whole time. Fixed by gating the signal's commit on a `just_verified` flag (`self._last_lockout_verify == current_time`, checked directly rather than trusting only the refresh call's own return value, since `_sweep`'s own periodic recompute can equally make the cache fresh "as of right now"); an unverified read still rejects the current request via its own count-based check, but defers the broader signal to a call that can confirm saturation. 1 more test (`TestRateLimiter` now 43); the full existing suite, including every genuine-saturation test, re-verified unmodified. See `docs/security-review/CI3-33-core-infra.md` (CI3-33-2d, CI3-33-2e through 2j, and "The structural refactor"). |
| **`POST /mfa/recovery-codes` is not retry-safe**                                                                                 | Open (LOW, 2026-09-01, security review AUTH-7)                                   | Every call generates an entirely new recovery-code set and overwrites the stored hashes (`mfa_regenerate_recovery_codes`, `auth.py`). A request that is retried after the server already committed — a network retry, a double-click, or (pre-`_verify_and_consume_totp`) the cross-endpoint TOTP replay AUTH-7 fixed — but whose _response_ was never seen by the client leaves the user without the codes they were shown: the stored hashes match a set that was displayed once and then lost in transit. AUTH-7's fix narrows one path into this (a retry reusing the _same_ TOTP code now fails cleanly with "Invalid verification code," since the code is already consumed, rather than silently generating a second set) but does not close the general case — a retry with a _fresh_ code still regenerates and overwrites. `mfa_verify_setup`'s one-time recovery-code display has the identical exposure. The right fix is an idempotency-key mechanism (or a short-lived "re-show last-issued codes" path) applied to every secret-shown-once response in this file, which is a product decision on request semantics, not something to guess at in an auth path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **HIPAA maximum password age is enforced only in the browser**                                                                   | 🚩 Open (MED, 2026-09-08, security review AUTH-15)                               | `HIPAA_MAXIMUM_PASSWORD_AGE_DAYS` (`config.py:176`, default **90**) has three readers and none of them refuses a request: `authenticate_user` logs a warning and returns the user anyway, `_build_current_user_dict` sets a `password_expired` flag on the response, and `get_session_settings` reports the number. The only thing that acts on it is `ProtectedRoute.tsx:166`, which routes the browser to the change-password screen. Its sibling control **is** enforced server-side — the `must_change_password` gate in `get_current_user` even carries the comment "the frontend honors the same flag, but the API must not rely on that" — and no equivalent gate exists for `password_expired`. A member whose password is 400 days old gets a full session from `curl`, any script, or any non-SPA client. **Why not fixed in the review that found it:** adding the gate locks people out on the day it deploys — on an installation that has never enforced this, potentially every member whose `password_changed_at` is older than 90 days at once, including whoever would have to fix it. Needs an owner decision on rollout (grace period, staged threshold, per-org opt-in, or accepting the cutover). See `docs/security-review/AUTH-01-auth-session.md` (AUTH-15).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Expired `sessions` rows are never reaped**                                                                                     | Open (LOW, 2026-09-08, security review AUTH-17)                                  | Session rows are deleted on exactly four events — logout, an idle timeout actually being hit by a request, a password change/reset, and refresh-replay revocation. A session that simply expires quietly (tab closed; access token lapses; the 7-day refresh token lapses) is never touched again, and there is no cleanup task: `delete(UserSession)` appears only in `auth_service._revoke_all_user_sessions`, and `scheduled_tasks.py` has no session job — though it does have retention jobs for the two neighbouring tables (`archive_expired_logs`, `expire_ip_exceptions`). Two slow consequences: every row keeps `ip_address` and `user_agent` with no expiry, in an app that sets a deliberate 7-year window on its audit log; and the table grows one row per sign-in per device forever, each holding two full JWTs. Neither is exploitable; growth would have degraded the hot `refresh_token` lookup too until AUTH-19 (same pass) indexed that column — it was the one token column missing an index, which this row originally missed. **Why not fixed:** a reaper needs a retention window, and that is a product decision — sessions are the data behind any future "where am I signed in" screen. It also means a new scheduled task, which is feature 31's surface. See `docs/security-review/AUTH-01-auth-session.md` (AUTH-17).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **A position manager can permanently demote a more privileged member**                                                           | 🚩 Open (MED, 2026-09-08, security review PERM-5)                                | The three user↔position assignment routes (`PUT /users/{id}/roles`, `POST /users/{id}/roles/{role_id}`, `DELETE /users/{id}/roles/{role_id}`) are gated on `users.update_positions` OR `members.assign_positions` and carry two guards, neither of which compares the caller to the target. `_enforce_role_grant_ceiling` walks the **incoming** roles, so it is a no-op on a removal (`role_ids: []` passes trivially); `assert_positions_retain_administrator` only asks whether _somebody_ in the org would still hold `members.manage`. The seeded **Secretary** and **Membership Coordinator** positions hold both assignment grants plus `members.manage`, and hold none of `settings.manage`, `security.manage` or `positions.*` — so either can strip the wildcard `it_manager` position from the department’s only `*` holder and the continuity guard still passes, because the remover themselves is the second administrator it counts. **It cannot be undone through the API:** re-granting runs the ceiling, which requires already holding `*`, and minting a replacement is impossible because `create_role`/`update_role` validate against `get_all_permissions()`, which contains no wildcard. `admin_continuity_service`’s own docstring says recovery from an administrator-less state “needs a database administrator”; this reaches the same place by a door that module does not watch. Reproduced against the real helpers. The neighbouring paths **do** ask this question — `_enforce_role_edit_ceiling` on a position’s permission list (ORU-7), `_enforce_account_reset_ceiling` on a credential reset — so the gap is an inconsistency, not a design. **Why not fixed in the review that found it:** the obvious guard (“you may not change the positions of a member whose effective permissions exceed your own”) also blocks the legitimate case a Membership Coordinator exists for — offboarding a departing chief — and the department that most needs the guard is the one whose chief has left. Three non-equivalent options are laid out in `docs/security-review/PERM-02-permissions-roles.md` (PERM-5): a full demotion ceiling, a narrow “last `*` holder” guard mirroring `assert_not_last_administrator`, or accepting the removal and giving `*` a restore path. Owner decision.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **`GET /api/public/v1/events/public` answers 500 whenever an events field is whitelisted**                                       | 🚩 Open (LOW, 2026-09-08, security review PUB-7)                                 | The handler builds `{title, description, start_datetime, end_datetime, location, event_type}` per event (`app/api/public/portal.py:482-493`); `response_model=list[PublicEvent]` requires `{id, title, description, event_type, start_time, end_time, location, is_public}`, all of them required (`app/schemas/public_portal.py:269-279`). Three names do not exist in what the handler produces. With no events field whitelisted, `filtered_event` is `{}`, the `if filtered_event:` guard skips the row and the route returns `[]` — which is why nobody has noticed. Whitelist one events field with one upcoming public-education event and FastAPI's response serialisation raises `ResponseValidationError` outside the handler's `try`, so the department's public website gets a 500 exactly when an administrator finishes configuring it. Fail-closed (generic 500 body, no leak), availability only. Needs a product decision because `docs/PUBLIC_API_DOCUMENTATION.md:207-220` documents the **schema's** names: re-keying the handler voids any `start_datetime`/`end_datetime` whitelist row already created and forces a call on whether the internal event `id` and `is_public` become whitelistable on an unauthenticated surface, while re-declaring `PublicEvent` to match the handler contradicts the published docs. See `docs/security-review/PUB-03-public-surface-webhooks.md` (PUB-7).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **A failed public-portal authentication is never recorded, so its anomaly detector cannot fire**                                 | 🚩 Open (LOW, 2026-09-08, security review PUB-8)                                 | `detect_anomalies` counts `public_portal_access_log` rows with `status_code == 401` in the last five minutes (`app/core/public_portal_security.py:322-334`), but nothing has ever written one: the only caller of `log_access` is `app/api/public/portal.py`, and `authenticate_api_key` raises its 401 from the dependency, before any handler runs. Writing one needs `PublicPortalAccessLog.organization_id` and `config_id` to be nullable — an unknown API key cannot be attributed to an organization, and both are `nullable=False` (`app/models/public_portal.py:202-211`) even though the model's own comment beside `api_key_id` says "NULL if invalid/missing key". That is a migration plus a decision about how an unattributable row is scoped for the per-organization admin read. The related half — error-path rows being rolled back with the request transaction — was fixed on 2026-09-08. Until then the per-IP limiter and `suspicious_ip` are the controls covering failed portal auth. See `docs/security-review/PUB-03-public-surface-webhooks.md` (PUB-8).
